keep one bad file from killing the whole run\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc()\n        return None, dict(session_id=session_id_from_path(path), path=path,\n                          rejected=f'error: {e}')\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,\n                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile')\n    g = ap.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n    g.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True)\n    g.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--jobs', type=int, default=min(32, os.cpu_count() or 8))\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\n    print(f'found {len(files)} NWB files')\n    n_sample = 2\n    if args.sample:\n        # keep scanning until `n_sample` sessions pass the session-selection\n        # criteria, so the sample is a usable mini-dataset\n        files = files[:8]\n        print(f'--sample: scanning up to {len(files)} files for '\n              f'{n_sample} accepted sessions')\n\n    # `--show-processing` plots the first two *accepted* sessions, so debug\n    # information is requested for a few more files than that.\n    n_debug = 8 if args.show_processing else 0\n    jobs = [(p, i < n_debug) for i, p in enumerate(files)]\n    n_plots = 0\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    results, infos = [], []\n    nworkers = max(1, min(args.jobs, len(files)))\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=nworkers) as ex:\n        for k, (res, info) in enumerate(ex.map(_worker, jobs)):\n            if args.sample and len(results) >= n_sample:\n                break\n            infos.append(info)\n            if res is None:\n                print(f'[{k+1}/{len(files)}] SKIP {info[\"session_id\"]}: '\n                      f'{info.get(\"rejected\")} (perf={info.get(\"performance\", float(\"nan\")):.3f}, '\n                      f'L={info.get(\"n_correct_left\")}, R={info.get(\"n_correct_right\")})',\n                      flush=True)\n                continue\n            results.append(res)\n            i = res['info']\n            print(f'[{k+1}/{len(files)}] {i[\"session_id\"]}: {i[\"n_neurons\"]} neurons, '\n                  f'{i[\"n_trials\"]} trials, perf={i[\"performance\"]:.3f}, '\n                  f'{i[\"t_total\"]:.1f}s (read {i[\"t_read\"]:.1f} / bin {i[\"t_neural\"]:.1f} '\n                  f'/ io {i[\"t_io\"]:.2f} / pack {i[\"t_pack\"]:.2f})', flush=True)\n            if 'debug' in res:\n                if n_plots < 2:\n                    make_processing_plot(res, f'/app/processing_{res[\"session_id\"]}.png')\n                    n_plots += 1\n                del res['debug']\n\n    t_proc = time.time() - t0\n    print(f'\\nprocessed {len(results)}/{len(files)} sessions in {t_proc:.1f}s '\n          f'({t_proc / max(1, len(files)):.2f}s per file, {nworkers} workers)')\n\n    # ---------------- assemble -------------------------------------------------\n    results.sort(key=lambda r: r['session_id'])\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_index = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    all_regions = sorted({str(reg) for r in results for reg in r['unit_region']})\n    reg_index = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(all_regions)}\n\n    data = {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_index[r['subject']] for r in results], dtype=np.int64),\n        'brain_regions': all_regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_index[str(x)] for x in r['unit_region']], dtype=np.int64)\n                             for r in results],\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n        'metadata': {\n            'dataset': 'Mesoscale Activity Map (MAP), DANDI:000363 v0.230822.0128',\n            'references': [\n                'Chen et al., Brain-wide neural activity underlying memory-guided '\n                'movement, Cell 187, 676-691 (2024)',\n                'Wang, Kurgyis et al., Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding '\n                'structured across and within brain areas, Nat Neurosci (2025)',\n            ],\n            'task_description': (\n                'Auditory memory-guided directional licking task. A series of three '\n                '150 ms pure tones (3 kHz -> lick right, 12 kHz -> lick left) is played '\n                'during a 0.65 s sample epoch, followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch during '\n                'which the mouse must withhold licking. An auditory go cue ends the '\n                'delay, and the mouse reports its choice by licking the left or right '\n                'lick port within a 1.5 s answer period. Decoded outputs are the lick '\n                'direction choice (left / right / no lick), the trial outcome '\n                '(ignore / miss / hit), whether the trial contained an early lick '\n                '(no / yes), and the discretised side-view tongue y-position per time '\n                'bin (<40th percentile / 40-60th percentile / >60th percentile of the '\n                'session, or not visible). Decoder inputs are the time since the tone '\n                '(sample-epoch) onset and whether ALM photoinhibition is on.'),\n            'time_bin_size': BIN_WIDTH * 1000.0,   # ms\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'go cue onset (auditory Go cue ending the delay epoch)',\n            'off_start': T_START,\n            'off_end': T_STOP,\n            'neural_units': 'firing rate, Hz (spike count per 50 ms bin / 0.05 s)',\n            'n_timepoints': N_BINS,\n            'species': 'Mus musculus',\n            'recording': 'Neuropixels 1.0 / 2.0, 2-5 simultaneous probes, Kilosort2 spike sorting',\n            'neuron_curation': (\n                \"units/classification == 'good' (quality-control classifier of the \"\n# Dataset Conversion Notes\n\n## Overview\n- **Dataset**: Mesoscale Activity Map (MAP) dataset, DANDI:000363 v0.230822.0128\n  (Chen, Nguyen, Li & Svoboda 2023). Data paper: Chen et al., *Brain-wide neural\n  activity underlying memory-guided movement*, Cell 2024 (`/app/datapaper.pdf`).\n  Methods paper: Wang, Kurgyis et al., *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding\n  structured across and within brain areas*, Nat. Neurosci. 2025 (`/app/methodpaper.pdf`).\n- **Date started**: 2026-09-19\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents (`/app`):\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` (this file)\n- `ChenLiuEtAl2023_SpikeSortingQC.pdf` — spike sorting / QC white paper\n- `datapaper.pdf` — Chen et al. 2024 (Cell)\n- `methodpaper.pdf` — Wang/Kurgyis et al. 2025 (Nat Neurosci)\n- `methods.txt` — excerpted STAR Methods\n- `code/` — `MapVideoAnalysis` repository (reference analysis code)\n- `data/` — 174 NWB files in 28 `sub-*` folders, plus `dandiset.yaml` (50 GB)\n- `decoder.py`, `train_decoder.py` — provided decoder / validation code\n- `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yaml`\n\nEnvironment verified: `python3` with numpy 2.4.4, torch 2.6.0+cu124, h5py 3.16.0,\npynwb 4.1.0. GPU: NVIDIA L4 (23 GB). 128 CPUs, ~1 TB RAM.\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/code` is the `druckmann-lab/MapVideoAnalysis` repo accompanying the methods\npaper. The relevant modules:\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `process_all_sess_parallel` / `process_one_sess` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | LOADING | Top-level ephys preprocessing: concatenates all probes of a session, reads behaviour/task variables, applies QC, splits into regions, writes per-region pickles |\n| `sliding_histogram` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Bins spike times into **firing rates** (`counts / bin_width`) on sliding bins; `begin_time`/`end_time` are *bin-centre* bounds |\n| `process_one_area` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Truncates spike times to `[begin_time, end_time]` (already go-cue aligned) then calls `sliding_histogram` |\n| `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | CURATION | Intersects the QC \"good unit\" index list with a hemisphere mask (`ccf_x >= 5700` ⇒ **left**) and requires a non-empty CCF annotation |\n| `preprocess_all_ephys.py` | `Sherlock/` | PROCESSING | Actual parameters used for the paper: `bw = 0.04 s`, `stride = 0.0034 s`, `begin_time = -3.0`, `end_time = 3.0`, `qc_mode = 'classifier'` |\n| `get_regular_trial_mask` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | CURATION | \"Regular\" trials = no early lick, no auto-water, no free-water, no no-response (`correctness != -1`), no photostimulation |\n| `load_session` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | LOADING | Concatenates the per-region firing-rate pickles of a session along the neuron axis |\n| `align_markers_between_lims` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns DeepLabCut markers (nose/tongue/jaw x,y) to the **go cue**, `t_min=-3, t_max=1.5`, `dt=0.0034`; takes the last video frame inside each bin |\n| `get_bad_trial_inds` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | CURATION | Flags trials where the number of video frames does not match the trial duration |\n| `nested_cross_validation` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | ANALYSIS | PCA(16) + logistic-regression population decoding, 5-fold outer CV |\n| `check_fr` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_utils.py` | CURATION | Drops neurons with zero across-trial variance |\n\n### Notes\n- This is **electrophysiology**, so no ΔF/F. Neurons *are* filtered by quality: the\n  reference uses the classifier-based \"good units\" list (`qc_mode='classifier'`,\n  `goodunits/` folder) produced by the QC pipeline described in the white paper.\n  In the NWB files this corresponds exactly to the `units/classification` column\n  (`'good'` vs `'unlabelled'`) — see the sanity check in Step 4.\n- Reference alignment event is always the **go cue** (in their exported `.mat`,\n  spike times are already stored relative to the go cue; `task_cue_time[0]` is the\n  go-cue time and lick/photostim times are shifted by it).\n- Reference neural binning: firing **rate** in Hz = spike count / bin width.\n- Reference region assignment: 14 major regions\n  (`ALM, Medulla, Midbrain, Striatum, Thalamus, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus,\n  Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum`)\n  × 2 hemispheres, hemisphere from CCF ML coordinate with midline at 5700 µm.\n- The reference code reads DataJoint `.mat` exports, not NWB. Every variable it\n  uses has a direct NWB counterpart (mapping table in Step 4).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data/sub-<id>/sub-<id>_ses-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS>_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb`\n— one NWB (HDF5) file per recording session, 174 files, 28 subjects, 50 GB.\n\nRelevant contents of each file:\n\n| Path | Shape | Meaning |\n|------|-------|---------|\n| `intervals/trials/start_time`, `stop_time` | (n_trials,) | trial interval in session time; **spikes exist only inside these intervals** |\n| `intervals/trials/outcome` | (n_trials,) | `hit` / `miss` / `ignore` |\n| `intervals/trials/trial_instruction` | (n_trials,) | `left` / `right` (instructed lick direction) |\n| `intervals/trials/early_lick` | (n_trials,) | `early` / `no early` |\n| `intervals/trials/auto_water`, `free_water` | (n_trials,) | 0/1 flags |\n| `intervals/trials/photostim_onset/duration/power` | (n_trials,) | strings, `'N/A'` when no photostimulation |\n| `intervals/trials/task`, `task_protocol` | (n_trials,) | `audio delay` task |\n| `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps` | (n_trials,) | **go cue onset**, session time |\n| `.../sample_start_times`, `sample_stop_times` | (≥n_trials,) | tone (sample) epoch on/offsets — more than n_trials because early licks trigger epoch replays |\n| `.../delay_start_times`, `delay_stop_times`, `presample_*`, `trialend_*` | | other epoch boundaries |\n| `.../left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times` | | individual lick contacts |\n| `.../photostim_start_times`, `photostim_stop_times` | (n_stim_trials,) | photostimulation on/off, session time; `control` = laser id (4 = unilateral, 6 = bilateral ALM) |\n| `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking` | (n_frames, 3) + timestamps | DeepLabCut `(tongue_x, tongue_y, tongue_likelihood)` at 300 Hz (dt = 3.4 ms) |\n| `.../Camera0_side_JawTracking`, `..._NoseTracking` | (n_frames, 3) | jaw / nose markers |\n| `units/spike_times` + `spike_times_index` | ragged | spike times in **session time** |\n| `units/classification` | (n_units,) | `good` / `unlabelled` — QC classifier output |\n| `units/unit_quality` | (n_units,) | `good` / `multi` (Kilosort label) |\n| `units/anno_name` | (n_units,) | CCF v3 structure name of the unit |\n| `units/electrodes` | (n_units,) | row index into the electrodes table |\n| `units/is_good_trials` | (n_units, n_trials) | per-unit per-trial validity flag |\n| `units/avg_firing_rate`, `presence_ratio`, … | (n_units,) | 15 QC metrics |\n| `general/extracellular_ephys/electrodes/x,y,z` | (n_electrodes,) | CCF coordinates (x = ML, y = DV, z = AP), µm |\n| `general/extracellular_ephys/<probe>` `location` attr | JSON | targeted region, e.g. `{\"brain_regions\": \"left ALM\", \"ml_location\": \"-1500.00\", …}` |\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files, all 174 files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total, `classification == 'good'`) | 69,453 |\n| Neurons (total, all Kilosort clusters) | 272,227 (good fraction 25.5 %) |\n| Neurons / session (good) | median 390, mean 399 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | 3–10 (mean 6.2) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | mean 546, range 264–800 |\n| Outcome fractions | hit 0.687, miss 0.165, ignore 0.148 |\n| Early-lick fraction | 0.114 |\n| Photostimulation fraction | 0.196 (168/174 sessions have photostim) |\n| Instruction fractions | left 0.485, right 0.515 |\n| Auto-water trials | 1,339 (1.4 %) |\n| Free-water trials | 2,450 (2.6 %) |\n| Video streams | all 174 sessions have `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` |\n| Video sampling | 300 Hz, dt = 0.0034 s, session-time timestamps, one contiguous segment per trial |\n\nObservations that drive the conversion:\n1. **Spikes only exist inside `[trial.start_time, trial.stop_time]`.**\n   `go - start_time` ≥ 2.11 s (3.1 % of trials < 2.5 s);\n   `stop_time - go` has a bimodal distribution — ~1.75 s for hit trials, ~1.8 s for\n   ignore trials, but only ~0.2–0.5 s for **miss (error)** trials, because an error\n   lick triggers a timeout and the exported trial ends at the last lick.\n   Consequence: for 15.6 % of trials the [-2.5, +1.5] s window extends beyond the\n   observed interval, and those bins necessarily contain 0 spikes. This is a\n   property of the published dataset, not of this conversion (verified: 100 % of\n   spike times lie inside the trial intervals).\n2. Go-cue times are ≥ 4.58 s apart everywhere, so the 4 s trial windows never overlap.\n3. Every session has exactly one go cue per trial, and at least one sample-epoch\n   onset before each go cue.\n4. `units/is_good_trials` is `True` for 99.88 % of (good unit, trial) pairs, and\n   99.88 % of good units are valid on *all* trials — negligible.\n5. All good units have a non-empty `anno_name` and a non-NaN CCF coordinate.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Subjects | 28 | \"data from 28 mice\" (methodpaper Methods); \"Mice (n = 28, Table S1)\" (datapaper) |\n| Sessions | 173 behavioral sessions | \"aggregated over 660 penetrations, 173 behavioral sessions, and 28 mice\" |\n| Neurons (total good) | 69,943 | \"the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions\" |\n| Good-unit fraction | 25.9 % | \"This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2\" |\n| Neurons / session | median 393 | \"Each recording session yielded simultaneous measurements from hundreds of neurons (median = 393)\" |\n| Good units by region | ALM 8717, striatum 7664, thalamus 12808, midbrain 7495, medulla 2928 | methods.txt, Spike sorting and QC |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130–785 | \"Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n| Correct rate | 84 %, range 65–99 % | \"with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%)\" |\n| Photostim fraction | ~25 % of trials | \"deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials\"; \"In a subset of randomly selected trials (typically 25%)\" |\n| Photostim epoch | last 0.5 s of delay (17 mice) | \"We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s)… photoinhibition always ended before the 'Go' cue\" |\n| Sample epoch | 0.65 s (3 × 150 ms tones, 100 ms gaps) | methods.txt |\n| Delay epoch | 1.2 s | methods.txt |\n| Answer period | 1.5 s after go cue | methods.txt |\n| Neural data time bin | 40 ms width, 3.4 ms stride | \"binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n| Behavior/video time bin | 3.4 ms (300 Hz) | \"High-speed videos … acquired at 300 Hz\" |\n| Bilateral ALM photostim effect | performance 83.2 % → 71.7 % | methods.txt |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: everything is aligned to the **go cue**; the reference\n  extracts [-3, +3] s (ephys) and [-3, +1.5] s (video markers).\n- **Binning**: firing rate = spike count / bin width (Hz).\n- **Video**: DeepLabCut markers for tongue, jaw, nose from the *side* view only.\n  Outliers identified by a five-sigma velocity threshold and imputed from nearby\n  frames. \"When the tongue was occluded while it was in the mouth, as was typically\n  the case before the response epoch, we set the tongue position to its mean value.\"\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (reference):\n- Kilosort2 clusters passing the region-specific logistic-regression QC classifier\n  (\"good\" units). ⇒ NWB `units/classification == 'good'`.\n- Units must have both ephys and histology (i.e. a CCF annotation).\n- For the *single-neuron variance-explained* analyses only: neurons with mean firing\n  rate < 2 Hz excluded, and neurons with R² < 0.01 excluded.\n- `check_fr`: neurons with zero across-trial variance dropped.\n\n**Trial curation rules** (reference):\n- Session selection (datapaper): overall behavioral performance > 65 % **and** at\n  least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials.\n  Performance = fraction correct among control (no-photostim) trials, excluding\n  early-lick trials.\n- Trial selection (both papers / `get_regular_trial_mask`): exclude photostimulation,\n  free-water, auto-water, early-lick and no-response (\"ignore\") trials.\n\n### Decoders Trained\nThe two reference papers do not train the decoder specified here, so there is no\ndirectly comparable accuracy. The closest reported numbers:\n\n| Decoded variable | Reported performance | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Lick direction (choice) from single-neuron delay firing rate | AUC > 0.65 used as the \"choice-modulated\" threshold; population CD<sub>choice</sub> decoding well above chance | methodpaper Fig. 7; datapaper Fig. 4 |\n| Trial type (video-prediction group) from single neurons | AUC > 0.6 / 0.65 thresholds | methodpaper Fig. 6–7 |\n| Behaviour (video) predicted from neural activity | population decoding accuracy ≫ chance | datapaper Fig. 6 |\nNeural-activity-from-video prediction is reported as R² (≈ 0.2–0.8 for the best\nneurons), which is not comparable to classification accuracy.\n\nBecause choice, outcome and (especially) tongue position are strongly represented\nbrain-wide in this dataset, well above-chance decoding is expected for all four\noutputs; tongue position and choice should be the easiest.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Reference `.mat` variable → NWB variable mapping\n| Reference `.mat` variable | NWB equivalent | Check |\n|---|---|---|\n| `task_cue_time[0]` (go cue) | `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps` | exactly n_trials per session, one per trial, in order ✓ |\n| `task_sample_time` | `sample_start_times` / `sample_stop_times` | duration 0.65 s ✓ |\n| `task_delay_time` | `delay_start_times` / `delay_stop_times` | duration 1.2 s ✓ |\n| `behavior_early_report` | `intervals/trials/early_lick` | |\n| `behavior_is_auto_water`, `behavior_is_free_water` | `auto_water`, `free_water` | |\n| `behavior_report` (1 correct / 0 error / −1 no response) | `outcome` (`hit` / `miss` / `ignore`) | |\n| `task_trial_type` ('l'/'r') | `trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`) | |\n| `behavior_lick_directions`, `behavior_lick_times` | `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times` | |\n| `task_stimulation` (power, type, on, off) | `photostim_power`, `photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration`, `photostim_start/stop_times` | onset field == event time − trial start ✓ |\n| `neuron_single_units` | `units/spike_times` (+ `spike_times_index`) | |\n| QC `goodunits` index list | `units/classification == 'good'` | good fraction 25.5 % vs 25.9 % reported ✓ |\n| `histology.annotation` | `units/anno_name` | |\n| `histology.ccf_x/y/z` | `electrodes.x/y/z` via `units/electrodes` | x∈[3100,8000] µm, midline 5700 ✓ |\n| `tracking.camera_0_side.tongue_x/y/likelihood` | `Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data[:, 0/1/2]` | |\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Number of sessions | n/a | 174 NWB files | \"173 behavioral sessions\" | 174 files, 28 subjects; `sub-456772` has two sessions on each of 5 days. The 1-session difference is in the published archive, not in our processing. Documented, no action. |\n| Total good units | n/a | 69,453 | 69,943 | −0.7 %; consistent with the one-session difference. No action. |\n| Units per region | 14 regions from CCF | see below | ALM 8717, Str 7664, Thal 12808, Mid 7495, Med 2928 | Our CCF-annotation grouping gives Striatum **7666**, Thalamus **12807**, Midbrain **7500**, Medulla **2921** — matches to ≤0.25 %. ALM gives **7346** vs 8717 because the reference defined ALM from a custom CCF voxel mask (`ALM_voxels_symmetric.npy`) that is not distributed; we use the Allen \"Secondary motor area\" annotation. Documented. |\n| Trials / session | n/a | mean 546 (264–800) | mean 476 (130–785) | Excluding early-lick trials gives mean **484** (209–695), which matches the paper's 476 closely; the paper's number appears to exclude replayed/early-lick trials. Our converted dataset keeps all trial types (required by the decoder outputs), so the mean stays ~546. Documented. |\n| Correct rate | n/a | 80.6 % over all 174 sessions | 84 % (65–99 %) | After applying the paper's **session-selection** criteria (perf > 65 %, ≥50 correct L and ≥50 correct R) → 148 sessions, mean **83.5 %**, range 65.1–97.0 %. Matches. ⇒ we apply the session selection. |\n| Photostim fraction | n/a | 19.6 % | \"typically 25 %\" | 168/174 sessions have photostim; within those sessions the mean fraction is ~20 %, and the paper says \"typically\". Acceptable. |\n| Photostim timing | last 0.5 s of delay (n = 17 mice) | in several sessions the laser is at [−1.2, −0.7] s re. go cue (early delay) | \"late (final 0.5 s, n = 17) in the delay epoch\" | Only a subset of mice used late-delay stimulation. We derive the stimulation window per-trial from the recorded event times rather than assuming a fixed epoch. |\n| Trial exclusions | exclude photostim / early lick / ignore / free+auto water | — | same | **Required deviation**: photostimulation is a *specified decoder input*, and early-lick / ignore(no-lick) are *specified decoder outputs*, so those trials must be kept. Free-water and auto-water trials are still excluded (they are rewarded irrespective of the animal's choice, so `outcome` is not a behavioural report on them). |\n| Neural bin | 40 ms / 3.4 ms stride | — | same | **Required deviation**: task specifies 50 ms bins. We use non-overlapping 50 ms bins (stride = width), which is the natural reading of \"50-ms-width bins\" and avoids leaking information across bins. |\n| Window | [−3, +3] s (ephys), [−3, +1.5] s (video) | — | — | **Required deviation**: task specifies [−2.5, +1.5] s. |\n| Occluded tongue | \"set the tongue position to its mean value\" | likelihood is strongly bimodal (≈1e-5 vs ≈1.0) | same | **Required deviation**: the task defines a separate \"not visible\" class (3), so occlusion is encoded rather than imputed. |\n| 2 Hz firing-rate cut | applied for single-neuron R² analyses | — | \"Neurons with low firing rates (below 2 Hz) were excluded from analyses\" | Not applied: it is a stability criterion for *per-neuron regression*, not a data-quality criterion, and the reference population-decoding code (`population_decoding_utils.load_session`) does not apply it. Discarding ~half the units would throw away real population information. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units/spike_times` (+ `spike_times_index`), `go_start_times` | `neural[s][t]` (n_neurons, 80) | align to go cue, keep [−2.5, +1.5) s, bin into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins, divide by 0.05 ⇒ Hz, float32 | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | rate, matching reference (`rate=True`) |\n| `sample_start_times` (last one ≤ go cue), bin centres | `input[0]` \"time_from_tone_onset_s\" (80,) | `bin_centre − (tone_onset − go_cue)`, seconds, float32 | — (decoder-spec) | continuous, time-varying |\n| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` \"photostim_on\" (80,) | 1 if the bin interval overlaps [stim_on, stim_off], else 0 | `task_stimulation` handling in `process_one_sess` | binary, time-varying |\n| `outcome` + `trial_instruction` | `output[0]` \"choice\" | hit ⇒ instruction; miss ⇒ opposite of instruction; ignore ⇒ \"no lick\". Values: 0 left, 1 right, 2 no lick. Broadcast over 80 bins | `behavior_lick_directions`, `behavior_report` | validated against first post-go lick (see sanity checks) |\n| `outcome` | `output[1]` \"outcome\" | 0 ignore, 1 miss, 2 hit. Broadcast | `behavior_report` | |\n| `early_lick` | `output[2]` \"early_lick\" | 0 no, 1 yes. Broadcast | `behavior_early_report` | |\n| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (`tongue_y`, `tongue_likelihood`) | `output[3]` \"tongue_y_position\" | per 50 ms bin: mean `y` over frames with likelihood > 0.5; 3 if no visible frame in the bin; else 0/1/2 by session-wide 40th/60th percentiles of visible `y` | `align_markers_between_lims` | time-varying |\n| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | | | |\n| `units/anno_name` + `electrodes.x` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | CCF name → one of 14 major regions (`region_map.py`); hemisphere from `x >= 5700` ⇒ left | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | region names are `\"left ALM\"` etc. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session curation** — keep sessions with behavioural performance > 65 % on\n   control (no-photostim), non-early-lick trials, and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and\n   ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials. *Rationale*: this is the explicit session-selection\n   rule in the data paper's STAR Methods; applying it reproduces the reported 84 %\n   mean correct rate (we get 83.5 %). 148 of 174 sessions survive.\n2. **Neuron curation** — `classification == 'good'` (the QC-classifier \"good units\"\n   used throughout both papers), plus a mappable CCF annotation. Neurons with zero\n   spikes in the extracted windows across the whole session are dropped (they carry\n   no information and match `check_fr`'s intent).\n3. **Trial curation** — drop auto-water and free-water trials (reference excludes\n   them; their `outcome` is not a behavioural report). Keep photostim, early-lick and\n   ignore trials because they are required decoder inputs/outputs.\n4. **No 2 Hz firing-rate threshold** — see Step 4 table.\n5. **Alignment / binning** — go cue = 0; 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning\n   [−2.5, +1.5) s; firing rate in Hz.\n6. **Tone onset** = the last sample-epoch onset at or before the go cue. Early-lick\n   trials replay the sample epoch, so this is the last time the instruction tones\n   were actually played before the go cue.\n7. **Tongue visibility threshold** = DeepLabCut likelihood > 0.5. The likelihood\n   distribution is strongly bimodal (median ≈ 6e-5 for occluded frames vs ≈ 1.0 when\n   visible), so any threshold in [0.1, 0.99] gives essentially the same labels\n   (fraction visible 0.1045 at 0.99 vs 0.1059 at 0.5); sensitivity checked in Step 10.\n8. **Tongue percentiles computed per session over all visible frames of the whole\n   session** — literal reading of \"over the session\"; using only visible frames is\n   required because `y` is meaningless when the tongue is occluded.\n9. **Per-trial outputs are broadcast over time** so that all four outputs share one\n   (4, 80) array, as required by the format (tongue position is time-varying).\n10. **Bins outside the recorded trial interval are left as 0 Hz.** The published\n    dataset simply has no spikes there; no imputation is possible and masking is not\n    representable in the format. Documented as a known dataset property.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Good-unit fraction ≈ 25.9 % of all Kilosort clusters (paper).\n- [ ] Per-region good-unit totals vs paper (ALM/Str/Thal/Mid/Med).\n- [ ] Median good units per session ≈ 393 (paper).\n- [ ] 28 subjects.\n- [ ] Session-selection reproduces mean correct rate ≈ 84 % (range 65–99 %).\n- [ ] Trials/session ≈ 476 after removing early-lick trials.\n- [ ] Choice derived from `outcome`+`instruction` agrees with the direction of the\n      first lick after the go cue.\n- [ ] Trial-averaged firing rate shows a go-cue-locked transient at t = 0.\n- [ ] Tongue \"visible\" fraction peaks right after the go cue and is ≈ 0 before it.\n- [ ] `time_from_tone_onset` is 1.85 s at the go-cue bin for ordinary trials.\n- [ ] `photostim_on` is non-zero only inside the delay epoch (always ends before go).\n- [ ] Output class fractions match the raw NWB trial-table fractions.\n- [ ] Independent spot-checks (`np.allclose`) of neural/input/output against raw NWB.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` (+ `/app/region_map.py` for the CCF annotation → major-region\n# Mesoscale Activity Map (MAP) — decoder-ready conversion\n\nBrain-wide Neuropixels recordings from mice performing a memory-guided directional\nlicking task, converted from NWB into the pickle format used by\n`train_decoder.py`.\n\n* **Source**: DANDI:000363 v0.230822.0128 — *Mesoscale Activity Map Dataset*\n  (Chen, Nguyen, Li & Svoboda, 2023), 174 NWB files, 28 mice, 50 GB.\n* **Data paper**: Chen et al., *Brain-wide neural activity underlying memory-guided\n  movement*, Cell 187, 676–691 (2024).\n* **Methods paper**: Wang, Kurgyis et al., *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement\n  encoding structured across and within brain areas*, Nature Neuroscience (2025).\n  Reference analysis code: `/app/code` (`druckmann-lab/MapVideoAnalysis`).\n\n## The task\n\nMice heard a series of three 150 ms pure tones (12 kHz → lick left, 3 kHz → lick\nright) during a 0.65 s **sample** epoch, withheld licking through a 1.2 s **delay**\nepoch, and reported their choice after an auditory **go cue** by licking one of two\nlick ports within a 1.5 s answer period. Licking during the sample or delay epoch\n(\"early lick\") replayed the epoch. On ~20 % of randomly interleaved trials, ALM was\nphotoinhibited for 0.5 s during the delay, always ending before the go cue.\nOrofacial movements were tracked at 300 Hz with DeepLabCut (tongue, jaw, nose).\n\n## Key statistics of the converted dataset\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| Sessions | 136 (of 174 source files) |\n| Subjects (mice) | 28 |\n| Trials | 70,949 (mean 522 per session, range 206–796) |\n| Neurons | 54,629 (median 392 per session, range 90–923) |\n| Brain regions | 14 major regions × 2 hemispheres |\n| Time bins | 80 × 50 ms, spanning −2.5 s to +1.5 s around the go cue |\n| Behavioural performance | 83.8 % correct (range 65.8–98.9 %) |\n| Pickle size | 9.45 GB |\n\nClass fractions (over all time points):\n\n| Output | Classes and fractions |\n|---|---|\n| `choice` | left 0.448, right 0.442, no lick 0.110 |\n| `outcome` | ignore 0.110, miss 0.153, hit 0.737 |\n| `early_lick` | no 0.884, yes 0.116 |\n| `tongue_y_position` | <40th pct 0.106, 40th–60th pct 0.053, >60th pct 0.106, not visible 0.734 |\n\n## Loading and using the data\n\n```python\nimport pickle\nwith open('/app/converted_data.pkl', 'rb') as f:\n    data = pickle.load(f)\n\n# neural activity of session 3, trial 17: (n_neurons, 80) firing rates in Hz\nfr = data['neural'][3][17]\n\n# the matching decoder inputs (2, 80) and outputs (4, 80)\nx = data['input'][3][17]\ny = data['output'][3][17]\n\n# which mouse, and which brain region each neuron is in\nmouse = data['subjects'][data['subject_idx'][3]]\nregions = [data['brain_regions'][i] for i in data['brain_region_idx'][3]]\n\n# time axis, in seconds relative to the go cue\nimport numpy as np\nt = np.arange(80) * 0.05 - 2.5 + 0.025\n```\n\nTrain and evaluate the reference decoder:\n\n```\npython train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl              # train + validate\npython train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only  # format check + summary\npython train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples # also save sample plots\n```\n\nRegenerate the pickle from the NWB files (≈36 s on 24 cores):\n\n```\npython -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full --jobs 24\npython -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing   # 28 mouse ids\n  'subject_idx':  (136,) int64,              # index into `subjects` per session\n\n  'brain_regions':    list[str],             # 28 'hemisphere Region' names\n  'brain_region_idx': [ (n_neurons,) int64, ... ],   # one array per session\n\n  'input_names':   ['time_from_tone_onset_s', 'photostim_on'],\n  'output_names':  ['choice', 'outcome', 'early_lick', 'tongue_y_position'],\n  'output_values': [['left','right','no lick'],\n                    ['ignore','miss','hit'],\n                    ['no','yes'],\n                    ['<40th pct','40th-60th pct','>60th pct','not visible']],\n  'metadata': {...},\n}\n```\n\n### Inputs (decoder inputs, time-varying)\n\n| # | Name | Definition |\n|---|------|------------|\n| 0 | `time_from_tone_onset_s` | seconds from the onset of the instruction tone series (the last sample-epoch onset at or before the go cue) to the bin centre; negative before tone onset. 1.875 s at the go-cue bin on 87 % of trials |\n| 1 | `photostim_on` | 1 while the ALM photoinhibition laser is on at the bin centre, else 0 |\n\n### Outputs (variables to decode)\n\n| # | Name | Classes | Definition |\n|---|------|---------|------------|\n| 0 | `choice` | 0 left, 1 right, 2 no lick | from `outcome` + `trial_instruction`: hit → instructed side, miss → opposite side, ignore → no lick. Constant within a trial |\n| 1 | `outcome` | 0 ignore, 1 miss, 2 hit | `intervals/trials/outcome`. Constant within a trial |\n| 2 | `early_lick` | 0 no, 1 yes | `intervals/trials/early_lick`. Constant within a trial |\n| 3 | `tongue_y_position` | 0 <40th pct, 1 40th–60th pct, 2 >60th pct, 3 not visible | side-view DeepLabCut tongue y averaged over the visible frames of each 50 ms bin, discretised at the 40th/60th percentiles of that session; class 3 when no frame in the bin has likelihood > 0.5. Time-varying |\n\n### `metadata` fields\n\n`dataset`, `references`, `task_description`, `time_bin_size` (50.0 ms),\n`temporal_alignment_event`, `off_start` (−2.5), `off_end` (+1.5), `neural_units`,\n`n_timepoints`, `species`, `recording`, `neuron_curation`, `trial_curation`,\n`session_curation`, `known_limitations`, `input_descriptions`,\n`output_descriptions`, `session_info` (per-session record: id, subject,\nperformance, trial and neuron counts, tongue percentiles, …), `raw_trial_index`\n(row indices into `intervals/trials` of each source NWB file, one list per session,\naligned with the converted trials), `n_sessions_available`, `n_sessions_kept`.\n\n## What was filtered, and why\n\n* **Neurons** — only units with `units/classification == 'good'` (the region-specific\n  quality-control classifier of the MAP spike-sorting white paper, which both papers\n  use) and a CCF annotation that maps to one of the 14 reference regions. This keeps\n  25.90 % of all Kilosort2 clusters, matching the 25.9 % reported in the data paper.\n* **Trials** — auto-water and free-water trials are removed (reward is not contingent\n  on the animal's choice there, so `outcome` is not a behavioural report), as are\n  trials whose behavioural video does not cover the full 4 s window. Photostimulation,\n  early-lick and no-response trials are **kept**, because they are required decoder\n  inputs/outputs.\n* **Sessions** — the data paper's session-selection criteria (control-trial\n  performance > 65 % and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and lick-right trials), plus one\n  session whose DeepLabCut tongue tracking failed. 136 of 174 sessions survive.\n\n## Known limitation\n\nThe published dataset only contains spikes inside `[trial.start_time,\ntrial.stop_time]`. On error (miss) trials that interval ends ~0.2–0.5 s after the go\ncue, so the last bins of those trials necessarily read 0 Hz. 15.6 % of raw trials are\naffected; no imputation is possible. list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\\n\\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\\n\\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'metadata': {\\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        ...\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Key Format Requirements\\n\\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \\n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\\n\\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \\n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \\n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\\n\\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\\n\\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \\n\\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\\n\\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \\n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \\n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\\n\\n## Python environment\\n\\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\\n\\n## Decoder Reference\\n\\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\\n- Optional arguments:\\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\\n\\n### Purpose\\n\\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\\n\\n### Consistency requirements\\n\\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\\n- Loading of data\\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\\n\\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \\n\\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \\n\\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\\n\\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \\n\\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference papers and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \\n\\n---\\n\\n## Conversion Workflow\\n\\n**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Each step has explicit completion criteria. Do not proceed to the next step until the current step is complete. Use the exact step names below when documenting progress in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n**STATUS TRACKING**: At the start of each step, update its status to `IN PROGRESS` in CONVERSION_NOTES.md. When complete, update to `COMPLETE`. This helps you and the user track progress.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 0: Setup and Initialization\\n\\n**Goal**: Set up the working environment and create the notes file.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. **FIRST**: Create `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` with the template structure shown at the end of this document\\n2. Verify you can run `python3` and import key packages (numpy, torch)\\n3. List the contents of this directory to understand what files are available\\n4. **REMINDER**: Do NOT look at any files outside this directory\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists with the proper template structure\\n- You have confirmed Python and key packages work\\n- You have listed directory contents in CONVERSION_NOTES.md\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 1, verify that `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists by running `ls -la /app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. If it doesn't exist, STOP and create it now.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\\n\\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at code in the `/app/code` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\\n\\n**Goal**: Understand how the original authors loaded and processed their data.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Look for documentation or README files in the `/app/code` directory\\n- Read the provided reference code bases to see how the reference papers loaded and manipulated the data\\n- Understand which functions are being called to read, curate, process, and manipulate the data\\n- For neural imaging data, does delta F over F need to be computed? \\n- For electrophysiology neural data, do cells need to be filtered based on quality? \\n- Note to file important functions, their inputs, and outputs\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented key functions and their purposes in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 1\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 2, update Step 1 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 2: Dataset Exploration\\n\\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at data in the `/app/data` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\\n\\n**Goal**: Understand the native data structure\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Identify how data is organized (all files, formats, hierarchies)\\n- Determine available variables and their meanings\\n- Check data dimensions and data types\\n- Look for documentation or README files\\n- Note the data organization and structure\\n- Note dataset size parameters such as the **total number** of subjects (mice), sessions, trials, and neurons\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented the data structure, file formats, size, and available variables in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 2\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 3, update Step 2 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 3: Reference Text Reading\\n\\n**Goal**: Extract key information from the papers and methods.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Scan the reference papers and methods.txt for important details about the data\\n- Look for and note to file:\\n  - Any information about dataset size (number of neurons, subjects, sessions, trials)\\n  - Fraction of data with different values, e.g. fraction of rewarded outcomes\\n  - Data processing, curation, and filtering\\n  - Variables important to experiments\\n  - Temporal alignment\\n  - Temporal binning\\n- Read the reference papers to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 3\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 4: Check for Consistency\\n\\n**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Compare your understanding of:\\n  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\\n  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\\n  - Analysis described in the reference texts\\n- Note and investigate any discrepancies\\n- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\\n  - Experimental and behavioral variables\\n  - Data processing\\n  - Data curation\\n- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\\n\\n**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 4\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 5: Mapping Planning\\n\\n**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Map source data elements to target format components:\\n  - Which variables contain neural activity?\\n  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\\n  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `/app/code` directory. \\n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\\n  - Brain regions recorded from\\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\\n\\n**Done when**:\\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n- You have documented all decisions under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 6: Script Development\\n\\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `/app/convert_data.py`.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Write all conversion code in the file `/app/convert_data.py`\\n- Script should run as **`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\\n- Include options:\\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\\n- Write efficient code:\\n  - Vectorize loops\\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\\n\\n**Done when**: `/app/convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `/app/convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\\n\\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt`\\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \\n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \\n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \\n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \\n3. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt`.\\n4. Analyze the output file `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`:\\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- `/app/sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\\n- `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 7\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `/app/sample_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\\n\\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\\n\\n**Investigate any issues**:\\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- Decoder training completes without errors\\n- Loss decreases over epochs\\n- Accuracies are above chance\\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 8\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\\n\\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\\n3. Run `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt`\\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \\n5. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_full_out.txt`. \\n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \\n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `/app/verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\\n\\n**Done when**:\\n- `/app/converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\\n- `/app/verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 9\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `/app/converted_data.pkl` and `/app/verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\\n\\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\\nRead through `/app/verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \\n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \\n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \\n- Document your sanity checks to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \\n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \\n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \\n- Fix any mismatches. \\n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\\n\\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \\n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \\n- Fix any discovered issues. \\n- Document your comparisons in `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \\n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 10\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\\n\\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\\n\\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \\n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \\n\\n**Done when**: \\n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 11\\\" with a table of accuracies\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\\n\\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \\n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \\n\\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \\n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \\n\\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \\n\\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \\n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\n**Report all checks and results** to `/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 12\\\".\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\\n\\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\\n  - Key decisions and rationale\\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\\n  - Validation results and tables\\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\\n- Create a user-facing `/app/README.md` summarizing:\\n  - Dataset description\\n  - How to load and use the converted data\\n  - Output format specification\\n  - Key statistics\\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `/app/cache/` folder\\n- Create `/app/cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\\n\\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\\n\\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. and video tracking\\n\\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). The instruction stimuli during the sample epoch were pure tones played at one of two frequencies (3 kHz or 12 kHz). Each tone was played three times for 150 ms with 100 ms inter-tone intervals. The sample epoch was followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch. An auditory ‘Go’ cue (carrier frequency of 6 kHz with 360 Hz modulating fre-\\nquency, 0.1 s duration) indicated the end of the delay epoch. Licking early during the sample/delay epoch triggered a replay of the epoch. During the response epoch (answer period: 1.5 s) mice reported the instruction by licking one of the two lick ports. Licking (consumption period: 1.5 s) the correct lick port triggered a small water reward (0.1 ~ 0.2 ul). Licking the incorrect lick port triggered a timeout (1-3 s). After mice stopped licking for 1.5 s, the trial ended, followed by a 250 ms inter-trial-interval. Early lick trials and no\\nresponse trials were excluded for analysis. Overall performance was computed as the fraction of correct control trials (i.e. no photostimulation), excluding any early lick trials. We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each. Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%; STAR Methods). During the delay epoch, mice maintain a memory of their choice and plan a movement.\\n\\nTwo CMOS cameras (CM3-U3-13Y3M, FLIR) were used to track orofacial movements of the mouse under IR light illumination (940 nm LED). The cameras were equipped with 4-12 mm focal length lenses (12VM412ASIR, Tamron) and a pixel resolution of 71 mm. High-speed videos from a side view and a bottom view (Figure 1C) were acquired at 300 Hz using software FlyCapture (TELEDYNE FLIR). We trained DeepLabCut 39 to track the movement of tongue, jaw and nose (Figure 1I).\\n\\nMulti-regional electrophysiological recordings\\n\\nWe designed a ﬂexible manipulator system for multiple Neuropixels probe insertions (https://www.janelia.org/open-science/manipulator-system-for-multiple-neuropixels-probe-recordings). Three to four small craniotomies (diameter, 1 \\u0018 1.5 mm) were made over target brain areas one day before the ﬁrst recording session. Details of recordings made with multiple Neuropixels probes in head-restrained behaving animals have been published (dx.doi.org/10.17504/protocols.io.8tphwmn). Target regions, insertions angles, probe types, etc. are summarized in Table S2. All coordinates are given with respect to Bregma (AP, anterior-posterior; ML, medial-lateral; DV, dorsal-ventral).\\n\\nExtracellular spikes were recorded using Neuropixels 1.0 probes, 2.0 single-shank (SS) and 2.0 multi-shank (MS) probes 3,4 (Table S2). Three to seven recordings were made from each craniotomy. Recording depth was inferred from manipulator readings (Sensapax uMp manipulators). After completion of probe insertion, brain tissue was allowed to settle for several minutes before recording. During recordings the craniotomies were immersed in saline.\\n\\nProbes were conﬁgured and data were visualized and streamed to disk using the open-source software package SpikeGLX (https://billkarsh.github.io/SpikeGLX/). Data from up to ﬁve simultaneously recorded probes were acquired at 30 kHz. Sync waves (0.5 s duty cycle TTL pulses) were recorded across probes and on auxiliary channels for synchronization.\\n\\nPhotoinhibition\\n\\nLight from two 473 nm lasers (Coherent OBIS LS/LX) was used to illuminate the two hemispheres of ALM. The laser power was controlled by analog outputs controlled by a Bpod (Sanworks). Photoinactivation of ALM (centered on AP 2.5 mm; ML 1.5 mm) was performed through clear-skull cap implant11 or via craniotomy by directing the laser over the skull (beam diameter, 1.8 mm at 4 sigma). Photoinhibition of the left-, right-ALM hemisphere, or both hemispheres was deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials 11,21 (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice). To prevent the mice from distinguishing photostimulation trials from control rials using visual cues, a ‘masking ﬂash’ (1 ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered using 470 nm LEDs in front of the mice throughout the entire trial. For silencing ALM, we stimulated cortical GABAergic neurons in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice. We used 40 Hz photostimulation with a sinusoidal temporal proﬁle (5 mW average power per hemisphere) and a 100 ms linear ramping down during the laser offset to reduce rebound neuronal activity. This photo stimulus is expected to silence glutamatergic neurons in all of ALM. 37 We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s), including the 100 ms ramp-down. Thus, photoinhibition always ended before the ‘Go’ cue.\\n\\nTo analyze the effects of photoinhibition, units with at least 10 photostimulation trials were selected for analysis (Figures 5 and S5). Bilateral ALM photostimulation reduced behavior performance from 83.2% to 71.7% (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice, n = 93 sessions). To exclude the effects of rebound population activity in the thalamus 16 and other subcortical regions, we used a 20-120 ms\\ntime window after photo stimulation onset to measure the effect of ALM inactivation in downstream regions (Figures 5 and S5). For ALM itself, we used 500 ms (i.e. the entire inactivation period) to calculate spike rate reduction.\\n\\nSpike sorting and quality control\\n\\nWe developed a data pipeline for preprocessing, spike sorting and quality control (https://github.com/jenniferColonell/ecephys_spike_sorting). The extracellular recording traces were ﬁrst band-pass ﬁltered (Butterworth acausal ﬁlter, 250 Hz - 9 kHz). We then sorted the dataset using both Kilosort2 32 (MATLAB version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/KS20_for_preprocessed_data: Release version of KS2 for preprocessed data) and Kilosort2.5 4 (Python version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/pykilosort: [WIP] Python port of Kilosort 2). Both sorting methods gave similar results. In the analyses presented in the paper, we used the output from Kilosort2. Each output cluster was associated with a list of 15 quality metrics calculated to assess isolation and sorting quality 7 (i.e. quality control) (https://github.com/AllenInstitute/ecephys_spike_sorting/tree/master/ecephys_spike_sorting/modules/quality_metrics).\\n\\nThe details of the spike sorting and quality control are described in an accompanying white paper (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2.\\n\\n[stdout]\\n# Setup\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport matplotlib\\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')  # Use non-interactive backend\\nimport
